So `column_totals` should return totals only for numeric columns, and for columns where all values are non-numeric, return empty string? Or return 0? The test expects `[\"\", 3]`.\n\nLet's examine `column_totals` function again:\n\n```python\ndef column_totals(rows):\n    if not rows:\n        return []\n    return [sum(r[i] for r in rows) for i in range(len(rows[0]))]\n```\n\nThis tries to sum `r[i]` for each column i. If `r[i]` is a string, `sum` will fail. We need to handle that. One way: check if the value is numeric (int or float), then sum, else treat as 0 or skip. But the test expects `[\"\", 3]`. That suggests that for the first column, since the values are strings \"a\" and \"b\", the total should be an empty string (or maybe the string \"TOTAL\" for that column?). So maybe the totals row always has \"TOTAL\" in the first column, and the rest are sums of numeric columns. The `column_totals` function might be used to compute the sums for the numeric columns, and then the totals row is constructed as `[\"TOTAL\"] + list_of_sums`.\n\nBut `test_column_totals_ignores_text_cells` expects `[\"\", 3]` for input `[[\"a\", 1], [\"b\", 2]]`.
